The `javascript:` scheme is dangerous only when the browser interprets the attribute value as a URL. Which attributes are interpreted as URLs?\n- href, src (in <a>, <link>, <img>, <script>, <iframe>, <frame>, etc.)\n- action, formaction (in <form>, <button>, <input>)\n- background (in <body>, <table>, etc.)\n- data (in <object>)\n- poster (in <video>)\n- cite (in <blockquote>, <q>, <del>, <ins>)\n- longdesc (in <frame>, <iframe>, <img>)\n- usemap (in <img>, <input>, <object>)\n- xlink:href (in SVG)\n- code (in <applet>)\n- archive (in <applet>)\n- profile (in <head>)\n\nAttributes that are NOT URLs:\n- title, alt, class, id, name, type, value, etc.\n\nHmm, this is a long list. The script\npreserves the original byte-for-byte formatting except for the removal of\nharmful substrings (script tags, event handlers, javascript: URIs, etc.).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\nimport sys\n\n\n# Inline event handler attribute (e.g. onclick=\"...\", onerror='...', onload=foo).\n# Also handles odd attribute separators like \"/\" used in <svg/onload=...>.\n# Must run before the more generic \"javascript:\" URI pass so that on* attributes\n# are stripped cleanly without leaving dangling URIs behind.\n_EVENT_HANDLER_RE = re.compile(\n    r\"\"\"(?P<lead>[\\s/])on[a-zA-Z]+\\s*=\\s*(?P<val>\"\"\"\n    r\"\"\"(?P<dq>\"[^\"]*\")\"\"\"\n    r\"\"\"|(?P<sq>'[^']*')\"\"\"\n    r\"\"\"|(?P<bare>(?!['\"])[^\\s\"'`=<>]+)\"\"\"\n    r\"\"\")\"\"\",\n    re.IGNORECASE,\n)\n\n# Strip the URI scheme from dangerous pseudo-protocols in attribute values.\n# We do not attempt to remove the whole attribute here; we just blank out the\n# scheme so the link can no longer be executed as JavaScript while leaving the\n# rest of the document formatting untouched.
